Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108 and PB-068 render each stripe once when qualifying a conventional PSD or PSB with a raw saved composite. Bounded plane readers compare the rendered white-matte RGB and alpha at the original precision. The shared matte conversion also serves the existing single-channel writers. Compressed composites retain their sequential comparison. Regression source checks raw plane boundaries and the shared compression header, alongside the existing native exact-composite cases. Runtime and open-time measurements remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108 and PB-068 add separate 64 KiB read-ahead windows to interleaved native PSD/PSB raw and PackBits color streams. PackBits row tables and payloads keep independent cursors. The importer admits mandatory tile staging first and uses direct reads under memory pressure. Row bounds, cancellation, native sample precision and final composite verification still apply. Regression source compares mixed-compression samples and underlying read counts with and without the windows. Runtime and open-time measurements remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108 and PB-068 make the indexed Photoshop digest scans sequential. Each SHA-256 or legacy section digest seeks once and streams through the existing 64 KiB buffer. The legacy section and composite hashes run only for files with supplementary PeerBrush sources. Full-file SHA-256 verification, length checks and exact saved-composite comparison remain required. Regression source covers fresh reads after a cached observation, short reads, cancellation and truncation. Open-time measurements remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 uses the same bounded Photoshop gradient-placement fallback in resident and native Open. A complete comparison first tries the original interpretation. After a mismatch, one document-wide integer-grid candidate can reuse the already staged raster and mask tiles. Only an exact native-depth match authorizes editing. Regression source covers wide PSD8 and PSB16 Gradient Fills and read-only protection after a one-word discrepancy. Runtime and receiving-editor qualification remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 and PB-068 separate supported Photoshop layer styles and the exact sRGB working profile from the total-file memory charge in Native Open. Descriptor sizes are checked before copying, and normalized effect settings are charged as they are retained. Other ICC profiles retain the protected preview path. PSB16 regression source checks a Color Overlay, a raster mask, the working profile and exact composite comparison above the old file limit. Runtime qualification remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 and PB-068 separate Photoshop Pattern Fill admission from total encoded file size in Native Open. Bounded pattern blocks, decoded tiles and each editable bitmap source are admitted separately from streamed raster channels. Imports apply the 32 MiB bitmap-source limit before adding another editable effect. A PSB16 regression uses an ignored resource to cross the old 256 MiB boundary and checks native editability, depth and offset. Whole-file verification and the exact native composite gate remain required. Runtime and dense large-document qualification are pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 retains right-angle Pattern Fill rotation at 100% scale in editable PSD/PSB imports and in both save paths. Resident source format 98 binds the angle. Native reopen verifies the exact standard record and keeps the older unrotated layouts. Regression source covers a rotated native-depth tile, mismatched composites, unsupported angles, combined offset and rotation records, and older-format protection. Interpolated scaling and Photoshop receiving-editor verification remain pending. [Adobe Pattern Fill controls](https://helpx.adobe.com/photoshop/desktop/create-manage-layers/layout-design-tools/apply-patterns-to-template-images.html)

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 carries bounded whole-pixel Pattern Fill offsets through PSD/PSB import, both save paths and Save Review. Import still requires an exact native-depth composite match. Resident source format 97 and native standard-record validation distinguish the new definitions from earlier raster fallback. Regression source covers signed offsets, fractional and composite-mismatch protection, and descriptor tampering. [Pattern descriptor reader and writer](https://github.com/Agamnentzar/ag-psd/blob/master/src/descriptor.ts#L1826)

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 saves eligible Pattern Fills with standard PtFl definitions and embedded Patt assets in both PSD/PSB writers. Patterns retain native 8/16-bit RGB and alpha. Save Review distinguishes editable Pattern Fill records from raster fallback. Resident source format 96 and Source36 readback verify the standard definition and bitmap against the editable source. Both writers retain current standard raster channels, masks and merged composites. Runtime and Photoshop receiving-editor verification remain pending. [Adobe file format specification](https://www.adobe.com/devnet-apps/photoshop/fileformatashtml/)

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 retains independent bitmap alpha in supported Photoshop Pattern Fills. The shared native-depth bitmap effect offers a Pattern controls coverage setting. Ordinary overlays retain their opacity behavior. Resident source format 95 guards the new interpretation. Regression source covers exact 16-bit samples, transparent tiles, raster masks, PSD/PSB reopen and source-format downgrade refusal. Standard PtFl output, runtime and Photoshop acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 opens bounded Photoshop Pattern Fill records as editable fills with their original opaque RGB bitmap. Unlinked placement, 100% scale and zero rotation/phase use the shared bitmap effect. Native sample depth, raster masks, asset ownership and the exact saved-composite check are preserved. PSD/PSB saves carry standard raster channels and editable PeerBrush sources. Regression source covers both depths and containers, reopen, mismatched composites and unsupported assets. Standard PtFl output, runtime and Photoshop acceptance remain pending. [Adobe file format specification](https://www.adobe.com/devnet-apps/photoshop/fileformatashtml/)

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068/PB-108 raises native animated WebP's encoded limit to 3 GiB. Held-header preflight still refuses oversized opaque static VP8. Open and Import read frame choices in one forward pass that hashes the exact source bytes without making a chooser copy. Decode revalidates the selected source through the authenticated snapshot and retains codec, work and storage limits. Regression source covers the native and resident encoded boundaries, exact chooser count, digest mismatch and no chooser disk allocation. Runtime and full-size platform acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068 decodes native animated WebP frame rectangles row by row. The shared RIFF parser provides checked VP8, VP8L and ALPH ranges from the authenticated file. Alpha and color use the same bounded row decoders as static WebP. Composition retains the existing pixel arithmetic and disposal, and full-canvas replacements can reuse staged frame tiles. Regression source covers animated lossless frame output above the former 64 MiB contiguous limit, mixed lossy and lossless parity, and cleanup. The 128 MiB animation file limit remains. Runtime and full-size acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068 native WebP animation can use canvases up to 16000 pixels per edge. The decoder admits one frame rectangle at a time and composes it into native tiles with the existing blend and disposal arithmetic. The 128 MiB encoded cap and individual frame allocation guards still apply. Regression source compares a wide tiled canvas above the former contiguous limit against buffered pixels for blended, disposed and lossy frames. Runtime and full-size acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108/PB-068 validates earlier APNG frames with Previous or Background disposal without creating temporary frame tiles. Background still clears its rectangle, and a full-canvas clear retires the prior surface directly. The selected frame keeps native 8/16-bit composition. Regression source covers dense discarded frames beyond the test store capacity, exact 16-bit samples after partial clearing, malformed compressed data and staging retirement. Runtime and full-size acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068 native Open, Import, file placement and paste admit APNG up to 16000 pixels per edge and 3 GiB per file. The picker and Image Info report explicit frame choices using bounded reads. The compositor reads the previous canvas and the overlapping frame in tile-sized windows, with 8/16-bit arithmetic and the existing ICC and orientation path. Sources that need no conversion reuse the composed surface. Regression source covers wide held Open and file import, an exact 16-bit blend across tile-backed frames and tile retirement. Inline and resident limits remain in effect. Runtime tests and full-size performance acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Resident and native APNG16 imports compose the selected frame at 16-bit precision. Native Open, file import, placement and paste share the admitted pass decoder and retain the frame's depth. Source copies exact samples and Over uses the shared native16 blend. Explicit selection, frame disposal, file verification and existing geometry and work limits still apply. Regression source covers exact 16-bit Source and Over values, Adam7, hidden default images and placement or paste into an 8-bit project. Runtime and large APNG canvas acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108/PB-068 validates discarded native GIF frames row by row without writing their tiles. Restore-to-previous leaves the accumulated canvas intact. Restore-to-background clears the intersecting rectangle when there is an accumulated canvas. The selected frame retains normal rendering. Regression source covers both disposal modes with dense frames larger than the test store allows, sparse selected output and staging retirement. Runtime and full-size acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068 admits animated GIF canvases and frame rectangles at the native 16000-pixel edge. Native selection decodes palette rows into tiles and bounds cumulative frame and canvas work. A full-size animation has room in that work budget for at least two full frames. Encoded file, frame-count, resident geometry and storage limits still apply. Regression source covers native-size metadata, explicit selection, transparent composition at the last tile and staging retirement. Runtime and full-size acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108/PB-068 checks the WebP layout from held headers before hashing or spooling files above 128 MiB. Ineligible animation and opaque VP8 refuse early. The picker pins eligible static files without making a separate chooser copy. Decode still checks the exact authenticated source. Regression source covers early animation refusal and snapshot binding after a seek probe. Runtime and large-file timing remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108 removes a redundant full-file admission scan when native Open or a pinned import already supplies an encoded source digest. The temporary copy verifies every byte against that digest before decoding. Unpinned inputs still compare two complete reads. Regression source covers the pinned and held paths, a digest mismatch and spool retirement. Runtime and large-file timing remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068/PB-144 gives native static lossless WebP import and export the same 3 GiB encoded ceiling. The native picker applies the file limit to VP8L, so large exports can reopen through the held-file path. Lossy WebP with separate alpha keeps 512 MiB. Opaque VP8, animation and resident files keep 128 MiB. Regression source checks the exact declared container limits. Runtime and large round-trip acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-147 checks Windows update history capacity before owner preparation and reserves the exact archive directory before final workspace close. The live owner retains and rechecks that slot through cancellation, installation and rollback. Regression source covers the last available slot, full history and unexpected contents. Native update qualification remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-158 adds an offline join for public, clean-install and upgrade evidence from Scoop, Homebrew, APT and Nix. It requires one version and source commit, matching definition and artifact hashes, and a common source archive. It also checks the APT signer and active validity, Homebrew publisher and Nix rollback. Regression source covers mixed packages, missing evidence and mismatched lifecycle claims. Public delivery and native qualification remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-158 public catalog readback now fetches and hashes the versioned release downloads used by Scoop, Homebrew and Nix. Scoop and Homebrew verify the selected install archive and the corresponding source ZIP. Nix verifies the pinned source ZIP. The verifier rereads the stable application tag and catalog branch before it issues evidence. Regression source covers missing or replaced downloads and a moved application tag. Actual publication and native acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068 streams static lossy WebP with compressed alpha. The VP8L row decoder supplies alpha residuals to an authenticated temporary plane. Native staging reapplies the original ALPH predictor while joining transparency and VP8 color rows. The temporary plane retires before adoption. Static WebP with separate alpha uses the 512 MiB per-file limit. Regression source covers compressed alpha parity across band edges and temporary-plane retirement on cancellation. Animation and full-size platform acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068 streams static lossless WebP output above 64 MiB. VP8L decoding keeps bounded LZ77 history and applies inverse transforms to consecutive rows. Predictor, color, entropy and Huffman group maps are admitted separately. The native writer retains exact RGBA samples and stages them within its existing store budgets. Regression source covers buffered pixel parity and a native image above the old contiguous limit. Compressed alpha and animation still have full-buffer limits. Runtime and full-size platform acceptance remain pending. [WebP lossless bitstream specification](https://developers.google.com/speed/webp/docs/webp_lossless_bitstream_specification#distance-mapping)

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068 streams lossy WebP with uncompressed alpha. The alpha reader uses two scratch rows and shares the buffered decoder's predictors. Native raw-alpha WebP uses a 512 MiB per-file limit so a 16000-square transparency plane can fit. Compressed alpha, lossless WebP and animation retain the existing encoded and decoder limits. Regression source covers all four alpha filters, truncated planes and full-size memory admission. Runtime and full-size platform acceptance remain pending. [WebP container specification](https://developers.google.com/speed/webp/docs/riff_container#alpha)

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068 streams opaque lossy WebP rows directly to native tiles. VP8 keeps three macroblock bands for filtering and chroma interpolation and one row of filter metadata. Encoded partition admission is unchanged. Regression source compares odd and even images across band boundaries with both upsampling modes and checks full-native-size allocation admission. Alpha, lossless and animation still use their existing full-buffer bounds. Runtime and full-size platform acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068 decodes static lossless WebP into one admitted RGBA pixel buffer. Opaque files avoid a second RGB output allocation and retain opaque alpha. Static native decode no longer reserves an unused animation frame buffer. Regression source compares decoder output and covers a wide native import below the previous allocation threshold. Animation and per-buffer limits remain in place. Runtime acceptance remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068 extends native static WebP Open, Import, placement, file paste and image patches to 16000 pixels per edge under the existing decoder allocation limits. Image Info reads the held container metadata directly. Animation keeps the resident geometry policy and its existing frame work budget. Images without a color or orientation transform keep the staged tiles directly. Regression source covers wide native open and pinned import, geometry refusal and unchanged animation admission. Runtime and full-size platform acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068 raises native JPEG's encoded-file limit to 3 GiB across Open, Image Info, Import, placement, file paste and image patches. JPEG file imports use the same per-file and native store admission as other tiled formats. Marker inspection checks cancellation between chunks even in densely stuffed entropy data. Resident and inline image limits stay in place. Runtime and full-size platform acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068 streams native JPEG decoded output above 64 MiB into the tile writer. The locked zune-jpeg version adds a row sink using its existing upsampling and color conversion. Single-scan baseline files can reach 16000 pixels per edge within the existing source and store budgets. Progressive and separate-scan coefficient admission stays in place. JPEGs without a color or orientation transform reuse their staged tiles. Regression source covers buffered parity, sink cancellation and a decoded frame above 64 MiB. Runtime and full-size platform acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108 draws AI activity brackets and human reservation outlines through the shared canvas projection. The frame copies reservation scopes under one engine lock and paints them after releasing it. The painter skips edges whose bounds miss the viewport, and only AI brackets near the viewport request pulse frames. Regression source covers a visible bracket arm with an offscreen corner under rotation and mirroring. Native frame timing remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108 streams selection outlines through the canvas projection and skips clipping work for segments wholly outside the viewport. Dashes keep their phase across hidden edges. Fully clipped outlines no longer schedule animation repaints. Selection and magnetic lasso gestures reuse the frame projection for input and overlays. Regression source covers clipping, dash alignment and preview selection state. Native frame timing remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108 reuses one canvas projection for tile drawing, selection outlines and pointer coordinates. Checkerboard and border share the same projected corners and skip drawing when clipped away. The viewport request still uses the same inverse mapping and pixel-boundary correction. Regression source covers rotation, mirrors, checker coordinates and an offscreen canvas. Native frame timing remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068/PB-143 adds JPEG XL to the shared image patch command for resident and disk-backed documents. The isolated decoder supplies source-orientation RGB samples and the patch command applies the existing clipping and blend rules. A native16 patch requires a native16 destination. Regression source covers the original orientation, profile handling, precision refusal and undo. Runtime and platform acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068/PB-143 native JPEG XL inspection and decoding now feed the isolated codec in 64 KiB input chunks. The worker retains a bounded prefix only when libjxl needs bytes from the previous chunk. It consumes the declared transport and checks EOF before completing. Native Open, Import, placement and file paste accept up to 3 GiB of encoded input within the existing source store and worker budgets. Regression source covers refills, early metadata results, truncated or trailing transport and the native file-size boundary. Full-size runtime and platform acceptance remain pending. [libjxl decoder API](https://libjxl.readthedocs.io/en/latest/api_decoder.html)

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068/PB-143 adds incremental JPEG XL worker output for native decodes above 64 MiB. The isolated worker sends partial scanlines in source orientation instead of registering a whole output frame. The parent uses an authenticated disk spool, bounds span metadata and replay work, rejects incomplete or overlapping coverage, and applies orientation with the shared native importer. That slice retained the original 64 MiB encoded-input cap and existing child codec, process, resident and disk budgets. Regression source covers the actual worker, native16 samples, orientation and malformed span data. Full 16000-square runtime and platform acceptance remain pending. [libjxl decoder API](https://libjxl.readthedocs.io/en/latest/api_decoder.html)

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068/PB-143 first extends JPEG XL Open, Import, placement and file paste to 16000-pixel edges under the original 64 MiB input and output limits. The isolated worker validates the inspected shape before output allocation. Native16 receive buffers hold a full wide RGBA row. Image information sends bounded file chunks to the isolated inspector. Regression source covers a wide RGBA16 codestream and protocol refusals. Runtime and platform acceptance remain pending. [libjxl decoder API](https://libjxl.readthedocs.io/en/latest/api_decoder.html)

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068 extends native JPEG Open, Import, placement, file paste and patches to 16000-pixel edges when the decoded output fits 64 MiB. Output shape is checked before coefficient or pixel allocation. The existing coefficient-plane, resident, disk and 128 MiB encoded limits still apply. Image information scans the bounded source without loading the file. Regression source covers wide and tall baseline JPEG, progressive and separate scans, pinned import and both allocation refusals. Full-size JPEG decoding still requires a row-output decoder. Runtime and platform acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068 extends native single-frame GIF Open, Import, placement, file paste and patches to 16000 pixels per edge. Full-frame, non-interlaced files reuse the decoded tiles directly. GIF animation retains its frame choice, resident geometry and work bounds. The 128 MiB encoded cap and native store budgets still apply. Regression source covers wide and tall files, interlacing, transparent palette colors, pinned imports and animated geometry refusal. Full-size runtime and platform acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068 extends native TIFF and BigTIFF Open, Import, placement, file paste and patches to 16000 pixels per edge and 3 GiB per encoded file. The selected page dimensions are bounded before decoder setup. The existing coding-unit, encoded intermediate, resident-memory and disk limits still apply. Image information reads bounded page metadata without loading the file. Regression source covers wide and tall RGB8/16 fixtures, original samples, orientation, pinned import and over-limit dimensions. Full-size runtime and platform acceptance remain pending. [TIFF format references](https://libtiff.gitlab.io/libtiff/specification/index.html)

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 retains Photoshop print placement and scale in the standard PSD and PSB Print Scale resource. Both writers preserve the original numeric bytes alongside print resolution. Supplementary sources must agree with the standard records. Malformed or duplicate scale records keep the original read-only, while valid settings carry through explicit flattened copies. Regression source covers resident and disk-backed paths at native 8- and 16-bit precision. Runtime and Photoshop round-trip acceptance remain pending. [Adobe file format specification](https://www.adobe.com/devnet-apps/photoshop/fileformatashtml/)

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 fixes print resolution in the compact PSD/PSB stream index. The index retains one record and at most one duplicate for validation. Photoshop saved, working and clipping paths now protect the original from editing, including files with supplementary PeerBrush sources. Readers can display the saved composite at its original precision without loading the path payload. Regression source covers both containers, both channel depths, private-source routing and resolution validation. Native execution and Photoshop acceptance remain pending. [Adobe file format specification](https://www.adobe.com/devnet-apps/photoshop/fileformatashtml/)

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-158 Scoop desktop acceptance now prompts the disposable VM operator to click Connect AI in the first installed generation. It checks the fixture client backup, migration and live stdio, then reuses the saved launcher after upgrade without another click. Offline receipt readback requires the new evidence. Native execution, reconnect through an installed AI client and public delivery remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-158 Scoop acceptance now opens the installed Start menu shortcut through Windows Shell after each installation or upgrade. The probe binds the returned process to the verified executable and requires an isolated workspace, a rendered frame and clean close. Receipt readback requires separate Shell and startup-document evidence. Native execution and public delivery remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 carries valid Photoshop print resolution through protected RGB and grayscale previews into explicit flattened copies at the original channel depth. Invalid or duplicate records add a warning and are excluded from the copy. Regression source covers resident and disk-backed workflows, precision and original-file custody. Runtime and Photoshop acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 retains the standard Photoshop print-resolution resource in PSD and PSB. Both writers keep the two fixed-point density values and display units. Malformed or duplicate resources and disagreements with supplementary sources protect the original. Regression source covers resident and streamed reopen, both channel depths and native container emission. Runtime and Photoshop acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 preserves Photoshop layer IDs on supported editable layer records, including separate opening and closing folder IDs, in both PSD and PSB. Copies omit both IDs. Duplicate IDs, unmatched dividers and disagreement between standard and supplementary IDs protect the file. Regression source covers nested folders, both channel depths, standard-only reopen, both writers and duplication. Runtime and Photoshop acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108 brush completion and sampled-color stroke admission validate the complete command while retaining only the projected endpoint and final pressure for the next Shift-click. Rendering still decodes all samples through the same validator. Regression source covers inline and parallel pressure, axis projection and malformed interior samples. Runtime and timing remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108 resident and native canvas previews share the stroke builder. Preview and submission serialize the existing point slice directly, including retouch offsets, without duplicating the point buffer. Existing pressure, straight-line, retouch and sampled-stroke regressions use the same command path. Runtime and timing remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108 resident canvas frames defer preview command serialization while a compatible full-detail render is pending. Frames that need a preview key build the stroke command once and reuse it for rendering. Reduced-resolution change tracking and compatible live progress stay active. Regression source checks skipped serialization and publication from the same live stroke. Runtime and timing remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108 layer drags reuse the hierarchy plan, command batch and destination label while the drop destination stays the same. A changed destination gets a fresh plan, and a rejected destination clears the previous commands. The drag retains document identity, revision and native generation checks. Regression source covers reuse, rejection and retry. Runtime and native timing remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108 layer display, range selection and drag previews build sibling lists once for each traversal. Multi-layer moves collect their roots in document order and insert them as one block. Regression source covers collapsed folders, multiple roots and changes to a parent with unchanged row order. Runtime and native timing remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108 layer rows index ancestors once per frame, build guide branches in one pass and format tooltip paths only when used. Idle frames reuse the collapsed-folder state and skip building a second row list. Regression source covers guide placement and tooltip ancestry during a drag preview. Native timing remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108 canvas frames inspect screenshot events by reference and retain only matching capture paths and image handles. Selection drags and releases reuse the input buffer when there is no shortcut to hold or defer. Shortcut replay still preserves original event order. Compile-only and runtime timing are separate checks.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068/PB-108 native BMP and TGA write bottom-up scanlines directly into their final tile stripes. The shared writer retains one-stripe memory admission and validates order, edge padding and completion in either direction. Regression source covers sparse stripes at both depths, exact samples across partial edge tiles, peak disk use and cancellation cleanup. Runtime and full-canvas measurements remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068 extends native TGA Open, Import, placement, file paste and patches to 16000 pixels per edge and 2 GiB per encoded file. Regression source covers wide and tall images, palette indices, packet extents, orientation and both native document depths. Runtime and full-canvas measurement remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068 extends native BMP Open, Import, placement, file paste and patches to 16000 pixels per edge and 2 GiB per encoded file. The row decoder retains palette, bitfield and RLE checks. Regression source covers wide and tall images, truncated data, digest-guarded import and both native document depths. Runtime and full-canvas measurement remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068 native Netpbm files can use 16000-pixel edges and a 3 GiB encoded cap. The shared row decoder, native memory and disk budgets, and import digest guards cover Open, Import, placement, file paste and patches. Regression source covers wide and tall rows, packed PBM, ASCII samples, 16-bit PAM and native precision promotion. Runtime and full-canvas measurement remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068 native QOI Open, Import, placement, file paste and patches now accept up to 16000 pixels per edge. The row decoder retains the 128 MiB encoded cap and uses the native memory and disk budgets. Regression source covers wide and tall decode parity, digest-guarded import, native16 preservation and opening at source depth. Runtime and full-canvas measurement remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-158 now has offline native receipt readback for Scoop, Homebrew, APT and Nix. It binds the selected lifecycle and desktop evidence to the exact release definition and source commit. Public readback and native execution remain separate, pending steps.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-158 APT acceptance adds an optional desktop run for each installed generation, including an archived upgrade baseline. The isolated non-root probe binds the desktop entry to the authenticated DEB, checks MIME recognition and opens the four retained Unicode PSD and PSB files through GIO. It checks native samples, editable sources and clean close. Native execution and public delivery remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-158 Nix profile acceptance adds an opt-in isolated X11 run through the verified profile desktop entry. GIO delivers four Unicode PSD and PSB fixtures after install, upgrade and rollback. The probe checks editable tabs and exact native samples, captures a workspace frame and closes only the authenticated installed window. Native execution, default associations and public delivery remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-158 Homebrew acceptance now follows each quarantined launch with a separate Launch Services document launch. Four Unicode PSD and PSB fixtures must open as clean independent tabs, retain editable sources and native 8- and 16-bit samples, and render before the app quits. macOS execution and public delivery remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-158 Scoop desktop acceptance now passes four Unicode PSD and PSB fixture paths at startup. It checks independent clean tabs and compares their editable source definitions and native 8- and 16-bit samples with the retained fixture evidence. Native execution, Windows Shell activation and public delivery remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 retains Photoshop's aligned-rendering marker on supported editable layers. Both PSD/PSB writers emit the original marker, and supplementary sources must agree with the standard record. Native8/16 regression source covers both values, invalid values and private-source mismatches. Runtime and Photoshop acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108/PB-118 Production Export passes rendered pixels from the shared export planner directly to the desktop texture. MCP and CLI still receive the PNG response with document coordinates. Native preview memory stays reserved until the panel hands the texture to egui. Regression source compares the desktop texture with the protocol PNG at both channel depths. Runtime and frame timing remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108/PB-118 Production Export compares document identity, revision, name, settings and selected targets directly while the panel is open. It builds a request only when starting a preview or batch. Regression source covers unchanged resident and native views, target changes and the canceled-render retry. Runtime and frame timing remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-118 Production Export refreshes the preview when settings return to their original values while a canceled render is still finishing. A late reply from that render cannot leave the preview stuck with a cancellation error. Regression source covers the round trip through the native export panel. Runtime acceptance remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-158 Nix acceptance now checks the desktop entry, Photoshop MIME definition and icon against the pinned source archive. It follows the desktop and MIME links through install, upgrade and rollback and checks that removal clears them. Native execution, desktop activation and public delivery remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-138 resident Save and image export now use a no-replace move when the destination was absent at capture. A file created by another application after the last validation survives the publication attempt, and the project stays open. Native8/16 regression source injects the competing file at the final move for PSD, PSB and PNG. Runtime and filesystem acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-118 native PNG and WebP export previews sample only the pixels needed for the bounded display image. They use the exact planned output coordinates and the existing compositor, resampling and color handling. Regression source compares the sampled view with full export rows at native8/16, including padding and both resampling modes. Runtime and performance measurements remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 preserves Photoshop's standard Lock All record on editable PSD and PSB layers. The shared unlock command clears it. Import checks that supplementary sources agree with the standard lock, and partial or unknown locks stay protected. Native8/16 regression source covers both writers, unlocking, and mismatched private sources. Execution and Photoshop acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-068/PB-066 native text placement and field controls now use the shared 16,000-pixel edge limit. Draft placement stops safely when native memory admission is refused and cancels if its captured generation changes. Regression source covers wide and tall fields at both native depths, refused placement and the actual preview command. Native execution remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-023/PB-110 clipboard key handling rejects Alt-modified Copy, Cut and Paste chords at both native event translation and document dispatch. The selection guard shares the same shortcut classification. Printable AltGr text can reach the editor on Windows and Linux. Regression source covers native event translation and selected pixel cuts at both depths. Physical layout and clipboard acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 accepts the modern Photoshop Indigo, Magenta and Fuchsia layer labels in standard PSD and PSB records. The layer menu, shared edits and observations expose the retained labels. Native8/16 regression source covers the standard-only round trip, unsupported values and native metadata edits. Runtime and Photoshop acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108/PB-157 native canvas capture reserves temporary command space only when it builds a hover or stroke command. Unchanged views can reuse the current request under resident-memory pressure. Pans still admit their changed request tags and preserve full detail by default. Opt-in quality tracking compares the request fingerprint directly. Native8/16 regression source covers these pressure boundaries. Runtime and timing acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-108 now scans and removes abandoned derived caches on an instance-owned worker after the listener starts. Shutdown cancels the scan before releasing the instance lock. The current cache and live caches with locked owner markers are excluded. Marker reads are bounded and the worker can also recognize legacy markers. Startup timing and native execution remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-158 Scoop acceptance now starts each installed generation from the verified Start menu target in a private desktop workspace. The probe requires a rendered workspace PNG and a clean window close after install and upgrade. Native execution, Shell activation and publication remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-158 APT upgrade acceptance can now restore a verified archived DEB from the current stage's immutable pool using its historical install receipt. The subsequent upgrade uses current signed metadata and a scoped unchanged keyring. A refreshed immediate predecessor can be pinned separately from the historical installed version. Native execution and public delivery remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-158 Scoop definitions now bind the GPL license and every third-party notice to the inspected Windows archive. Native install and upgrade verification check those exact bytes alongside the executable and source ZIP, and removal checks the entire installed app tree. Older Scoop receipts remain usable for catalog-byte readback and require regeneration for native acceptance. Runtime and publication remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Native canvas texture streams now reserve stripe slots for one texture band and its filtering neighbors. A 16000-square frame with an 8192-pixel texture limit reserves 16 stripe slots instead of 16384. Optional fade admission counts this index when it will stream its upload. Regression source covers the full-size allocation and reuses existing pixel parity coverage across stripe seams. Native timing and runtime acceptance are pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Unclipped paint layers with Color, Gradient or Pattern Overlay stacks now support RGB blend exclusions. Resident source format 73 retains both the editable stack and channel settings. Standard PSD and PSB raster records carry the rendered overlays and `brst`. Native8/16 regression source covers translucent backdrops, all channels disabled, clipping protection and reopening with or without the PeerBrush source. Runtime execution and Photoshop round trips are pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Pass Through folders without effects apply RGB exclusions to child writes in both CPU paths and GPU previews. Nested exclusions combine, and adjustment preparation sees the restricted backdrop. Resident source format 72 binds the mode and standard `brst` block. Regression source covers a nested Threshold adjustment, resetting restrictions, and PSD/PSB reopening at both depths. Native execution and Photoshop round trips are pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. RGB blend channels now work in clipping stacks over grouped paint and Solid Color bases without effects. Member exclusions preserve the stack's premultiplied values, including when Blend If changes visible coverage. Base exclusions preserve the outside backdrop at the final merge. Resident source format 71 binds clipped channel metadata. Regression source covers both backdrops, all channels disabled on a base, native8/16 PSD/PSB reopening and GPU parity. Device execution and Photoshop round trips are pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. RGB blend channel switches preserve the backdrop's premultiplied contribution in CPU and GPU composition. The layer context menu and shared command edit the standard PSD/PSB `brst` block. Resident source format 70 binds the block to the standard record. Regression source covers native8/16 translucent backdrops, all channels disabled, native source retention and invalid channel protection. GPU device and Photoshop round trips are pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. GPU previews now keep original clipping shape and visible coverage separate for supported ranged bases. Clipped paint can restore coverage, and clipped adjustments keep the resulting alpha. The device regression source compares native8/16 output with the CPU, including a hidden base, multiple restoring members, adjustment and empty shape. WGSL validation and SPIR-V translation passed. Native device execution remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Blend If on supported grouped paint and Solid Color bases now keeps clipping shape separate from visible coverage. Paint members can restore visibility inside the shape, and clipped adjustments read the current gated stack. Resident source format 69 identifies base ranges. Regression source covers partial coverage, standard PSD/PSB reopening and native regional parity. Previews of ranged clipping stacks use CPU composition until the shader tracks both coverage values. Native execution and Photoshop round trips are pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Grouped paint and Solid Color bases without effects support Blend If on clipped paint, Solid Color and adjustment members. The member's gate reads the stack's color and the base's effective coverage without changing the clipping matte. Resident source format 68 identifies clipped member ranges. Regression source covers partial base coverage, transparent pixels, native8/16 standard reopening, regional rendering and GPU parity. Blend If on clipping bases and native acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Active Blend If ranges isolate Pass Through folders without effects while preserving the selected mode in the document and standard PSD/PSB opening record. Child adjustments now stop at that folder boundary in both CPU paths and GPU previews. Clearing the ranges restores ordinary Pass Through. Resident source format 67 binds the Pass Through divider and ranges. Regression source covers nested adjustments, reset, regional rendering and standard reopening at both depths. Native execution and Photoshop round trips are pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Blend If now gates unclipped adjustments using adjusted color and the original backdrop. Both CPU paths and eligible GPU previews apply the gate before mixing the adjustment. Supported standard adjustment records retain the ranges, including imported adjustments without raster channels. Resident source format 66 identifies editable adjustment ranges. Regression source covers native8/16 Invert import and standard reopening, regional preparation and GPU parity. Native execution and Photoshop round trips are pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Isolated folders without effects can use Blend If against their composited children and the outside backdrop. The standard reader accepts bounded RGB ranges on folders with no raster color channels and protects active ranges on closing records. Resident source format 65 binds the ranges and blend to the opening folder record. Regression source covers both depths, original children, standard PSD/PSB reopen and native merged rows. Photoshop and GPU device acceptance are pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Unclipped paint and Solid Color layers without effects can combine Blend If with supported blend modes and partial Fill. Blend If changes coverage while the special Fill kernel receives the original Fill byte. GPU previews carry both settings without adding node fields. Regression source covers Multiply and Linear Dodge, transparent and opaque backdrops, native saved rows and GPU parity at both depths. Native execution and Photoshop round trips are pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. GPU-eligible previews can evaluate Blend If without expanding the node buffer. Integer cutoffs preserve native-depth threshold decisions, and the existing CPU repair handles output rounding near half samples. Added GPU parity regression source for hard and split Gray/RGB ranges at both depths, with transparent, partial and opaque backdrops. Native device acceptance is pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 adds Gray/RGB Blend If with hard and split source and underlying ranges on unclipped Normal paint and Solid Color layers at full Fill with no effects. The layer footer and shared command preview the current composition. Both PSD/PSB writers preserve standard range records and native-depth raster samples. Resident source format 64 binds ranges to the standard record. Standard imports require an exact merged-composite match. Regression source covers native8/16 coverage, standard records, original channels and protection on mismatch. Photoshop round trips are pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 preserves disabled Blend Clipped Layers As Group on supported Normal paint and Solid Color clipping bases at full Fill. The layer context menu and shared command can restore grouping. Both PSD/PSB writers preserve one standard flag, and resident source format 63 binds the stored value to that record. Exact composite validation and shared edit checks protect unsupported disabled-base combinations. Regression source covers plain Normal bases at both depths, standard reopen, undo and native record emission.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. RGB16 previews can use the GPU for all eight special Fill modes, including grouped clipping. The shader corrects wide Color Burn and Color Dodge quotients using exact base-65536 comparisons, retains native channel words, and uses the existing CPU repair for borderline output rounding. The GPU regression source now compares both depths across all eight modes. Shader validation and arithmetic checks are separate from device execution.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 extends partial Fill to grouped Photoshop clipping. Followers apply their Fill inside the base's original alpha, and the base applies its Fill when the combined group blends into the document. The resident and disk-backed compositors and RGB8 shader share this order. PSD/PSB keep both standard Fill tags and the original raster channels. Regression source covers both native depths, the transparent exterior, standard save and reopen, and RGB8 GPU parity. Partial Fill with effects and disabled Blend Clipped Layers As Group remain protected.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 extends partial Fill to all eight special Photoshop blends on unclipped layers without effects. The native CPU paths use the Fill-adjusted overlap color and separate source-only coverage at both depths. RGB8 has an integer shader implementation; RGB16 uses shared CPU preview. PSD/PSB preserve the standard Fill byte and require exact saved-composite agreement before editable import. Regression source covers all eight modes and partial-alpha overlap.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-122 keeps Photoshop Fill opacity separate from overall layer opacity for supported unclipped paint and Solid Color layers. The inspector and shared edit command preview the value without changing source pixels. Both PSD/PSB writers retain the standard Fill tag, the original channels and the current merged composite. Exact import validation and transactional edit checks protect unsupported combinations with effects, clipping and special blend modes.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. PB-158 Nix Connect AI recognizes verified custom profiles from an absolute launch path or an unambiguous PATH fallback. The launcher must match the running executable or its recognized wrapper, and the profile must link through its own generation to a store profile tree. The saved path follows upgrades and rollbacks. The native harness can exercise both discovery paths with a named profile. Linux execution and public delivery remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Darker Color, Lighter Color, Hue, Saturation, Color and Luminosity are available as editable RGB layer blends. The CPU and GPU paths use native sample units, and both PSD/PSB writers preserve the standard blend mode and original channels. Conventional imports still require exact saved-composite agreement. Regression source covers gamut clipping, luma ties, near-gray 16-bit channels, standard save and reopen, and protection on composite mismatch.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Vivid Light, Pin Light and Hard Mix are editable layer blends at both native depths. The CPU and GPU share the same native sample rules, and both PSD/PSB writers retain the original layer channels and blend records. Conventional import requires exact saved-composite agreement. Regression source covers native sample thresholds and rounding, PSD/PSB reopen and protection on composite mismatch.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Linear Light is available as an editable layer blend at both native depths. The resident and disk-backed PSD/PSB writers share the standard `lLit` mapping. The shared CPU compositor and GPU preview use the native-depth midpoint, and conventional import requires exact saved-composite agreement. Nondefault Photoshop fill opacity remains protected. Regression source covers native samples, standard save and reopen, and protection on composite mismatch.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Resident canvas capture now waits for the full-resolution frame after an opt-in reduced preview, matching the native capture policy. Reduced frames still appear during editing and update the on-canvas selection. Regression source covers capture readiness before and after refinement at both channel depths, including switching the preference off during a queued preview.

Unreleased PB-157/PB-108 follow-through, 2026-11-10. An accepted full-detail resident frame retires any older AI fade. Interrupting a resident fade invalidates its intermediate frame and requests the committed image again, even at the same revision. Native full-detail viewports retain pan buffering with the reduced-preview preference enabled after editing settles, including while the blue AI highlight remains. Regression source exercises shared resident edits, actual preview-worker publication, capture readiness and native viewport request geometry at both depths. Runtime acceptance and performance measurements are pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Paint-driven frame expansion now samples off-frame masks through fractional affine, Perspective and Warp placements in resident and native documents. A pixel edit establishes a new integer-grid original from the retained projection and the exact current mask samples. Undo keeps the earlier source and placement. Projection uses the existing geometry and work limits. Regression source covers both storage paths and channel depths. Runtime acceptance remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Resident paint-driven frame expansion now reveals translated mask originals outside the old mask frame while preserving current pixels inside it. After the shared edit applies selection coverage, only changed samples are written back to the retained original. An edit that would exceed the resident retained-source limit is refused and rolled back. Regression source covers resident8/16 preview and commit parity, erasing a newly exposed pixel, and moving an unlinked layer to reveal the retained off-canvas sample. Runtime acceptance and non-integer placements remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Native paint-driven frame expansion projects an integer-translated mask original into the newly exposed area. Current mask pixels, including transparent samples, take precedence inside the old frame. Brush and companion-plane staging, Smudge's selection baseline, and retained-original writeback share this projection. Regression source covers color-driven growth and erasing a newly exposed sample at both native depths, with unchanged history during preview. Native execution and the resident path remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Native pixel edits update a translated off-frame paint mask's retained original in the same transaction. The update compares exact before and after samples, reuses untouched original tiles and keeps pixels beyond the current layer. If the required original would exceed native geometry, the edit is refused before publication. Regression source covers native8/16 painting, standard PSD/PSB save, reopen, and an unlinked move that reveals a retained sample. Resident masks, other retained placements and immediate projection during frame expansion still need follow-through. Runtime acceptance remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Disk-backed Photoshop imports can retain off-frame raster masks within the existing native geometry limits, including 16,000-pixel edges. The importer and native standard-channel writer apply the same limits to the mask and layer union. Original mask samples remain in backed storage and the complete saved composite must still match. Resident imports and saves use their existing limits. Regression source covers exact native8/16 samples, expanded standard mask bounds, and native PSD/PSB save and reopen. Large-document runtime acceptance remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Supported Photoshop rendered and real raster-mask channels can have different rectangles. The resident and indexed readers compare all nondefault native samples in document coordinates, including off-frame samples, before adopting the real user mask. The shared default and flags, preserved no-op vector mask and exact saved composite remain required. Both writers retain the real raster data and regenerate the standard paired channels. Regression source covers differently padded PSD/PSB masks at both depths, off-frame disagreement, and save/reopen. Photoshop receiving-editor acceptance remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Native canvas requests hash live command metadata once per capture. Full-resolution and interim requests reuse that digest state and add their own display settings. The admitted copy still verifies exact JSON bytes. An unchanged request can still skip copying. Regression source compares the new fingerprints with the original JSON tuple at both quality levels, including a shared source command. Desktop latency remains unmeasured.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. The workspace captures the document, saved revision, recovery-menu availability and first reservation label together. Drawing those controls no longer takes separate engine locks or copies the reservation list. The closed AI tasks menu counts pending proposals without building the full history, task details or layer-name map. The main snapshot, AI tasks menu and preview request use nonblocking acquisition. A busy engine leaves the current preview request intact and schedules a retry. Regression source holds the engine lock across a preview request and checks that scheduling resumes when it is released. Desktop input and presentation latency remain unmeasured.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. W/E/R handles use the current selection bounds immediately. A native drag stays bound to the document generation observed at press, through preview and commit. The native worker uses the same original selection for each layer in a shared transform gesture, then moves the selection once. A repeated target begins a new gesture. Regression source covers Magic Wand followed by W/E/R drags at both native depths and a multi-layer move through preview, commit and history. Compilation passed. Runtime acceptance remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Selection-dependent shortcuts issued on mouse release or while a native selection is applying wait for that exact worker result. Replay checks the committed source generation, active project, layer, channel and tool, and uses the original key modifiers. Focus loss, cancellation, a changed source and empty selection suppress the replay. This avoids falling through to whole-layer actions. Regression source drives native Magic Wand release with Copy and W/E/R at both depths, checks pasted bounds and original-depth samples, and checks that queued Delete leaves the layer intact after an empty selection. Native desktop acceptance remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Native paint previews and commits reuse tiles through supported pointwise effects and finite blur or bloom stages. The damage rectangle expands by the regional kernels' combined reach, including effects that sample a group or adjustment backdrop. Pans can also reuse pixels through supported regional document filters. Unknown effects and stages that require global preparation use regular rendering. Regression source compares fresh and reused pixels at both depths with a layer blur, a document blur and a pointwise clamp. GPU behavior and end-to-end latency remain unverified.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. A successful native raster commit publishes the guarded damage bounds as temporary display metadata. The next full-resolution canvas render can reuse tiles from the last committed or live frame when it shares the same base generation. It recomposites the changed area and retains matching texture handles when those pixels also match. A successful reuse presents a complete texture set without collecting a second CPU frame. History movement, source replacement and commits without a raster bound discard the record. Animation and ordinary fallback paths use their existing render routes. Regression source covers a paint commit that matches the last preview, unchanged texture handles, one history entry and Undo clearing the damage record. GPU and latency acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Native paint previews can reuse display tiles outside the actual changed samples recorded by the guarded raster worker. Successive previews from one committed revision exclude the damage from both gestures, so pixels from a previous preview are repainted when the gesture moves. Tile borders count in the comparison. The path covers unprofiled full-resolution composites with direct color writes or pointwise mask writes and no active layer or document effects. Reframes, shared raster slots, spatial mask edits and mixed batches without a complete raster bound use normal rendering. Regression source compares exposed samples at both depths and checks tile identity, source retirement and unchanged history. Live GPU and input-latency acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Committed native views above the CPU frame limit can reuse GPU tiles during an aligned pan. A tile matches only when its complete filtering border covers the same document pixels from the same full-resolution source generation. The renderer streams only the columns needed by replacement tiles, including adjacent border samples. The worker retains no full CPU frame. ICC profiles, active document filters and derived effects use the regular streaming path. Regression source covers exact exposed pixels at both depths, generation changes, reused handles and crop alignment above the CPU frame limit. GPU and end-to-end latency acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Full-resolution native pans can reuse the overlapping rows and columns of the last published committed frame. Aligned vertical stripes still share their existing pixel owners. Horizontal and diagonal moves copy the shared portion into the new frame and composite only the exposed columns. The renderer checks the source, sample grid, profile and display status before reusing pixels. ICC-converted rows require unchanged horizontal bounds. Regression source compares both depths against fresh rendering for pans in both directions, wider crops, disjoint crops and reduced sampling. End-to-end pan latency remains unmeasured.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Native texture uploads convert contiguous rows, with one cancellation and footprint check per row. Cross-project hover uploads skip content hashes because those textures have no reuse path. Regular canvas uploads retain reuse hashes, including the filtering border. In an optimized Windows CPU probe on an Intel Core i7-14650HX, the median of 11 paired runs over 128 tiles of 131,072 pixels fell from 56.8 to 26.5 ms with opaque alpha and from 88.0 to 44.5 ms with mixed alpha. Both conversion loops produced identical pixels. The probe isolates conversion and does not measure GPU upload, presentation or input latency. Regression source covers exact filtering-border samples, cancellation and admission refund, and uploads with and without reuse hashes.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Combined linear Photoshop Levels can open as editable RGB8 adjustments when the complete saved composite matches. Each channel rounds to a byte before the master stage, and ties round upward. The mapping matched 16,000 opaque channel samples from a Photoshop-authored fixture. The inspector edits endpoints in byte units. Both PSD writers emit the supported standard record, with resident source format 61 and native Levels marker 4 binding the mapping. Combined nonlinear and RGB16 imports remain protected. Regression source covers the half-byte boundary, source downgrade, composite refusal, and PSD/PSB record readback. Receiving-editor acceptance remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Photoshop Levels import accepts zero alignment padding inside a neutral extended `Lvls` record. Four saved Photoshop tags with 62 records establish the padded layout. The reader checks every extra record and rejects malformed padding. The tested nonlinear combined rendering rules differ from the saved composite. Regression source covers the observed 632-byte layout and malformed records.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Disk-backed cross-project hover previews stream through the canvas tile uploader. The worker releases CPU image batches as the renderer consumes them and presents the finished tiles only while the original transfer is current. The desktop path can request the streamed canvas grid up to 16,000 pixels per edge. MCP previews return bounded PNGs with document coordinates. Regression source compares the streamed pixels with the pixels used for MCP previews and checks that hover leaves history and disk usage unchanged. GPU and latency acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Resident canvas frames and cross-project previews split into textures when the device cannot hold the frame in one texture. Tile borders retain adjacent samples for filtering. Regional updates refresh every tile that uses the changed pixels. Cross-project rendering requests native detail within its existing render budget independently of the texture-side limit. Regression source checks full width, a far-edge sample and a pixel patch across a tile seam. GPU and latency acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. An explicit `discover --connect` command uses the app's Connect AI launcher and backup rules for an authenticated running workspace. Scoop, Homebrew, apt and Nix harnesses register a private client fixture once and use its saved stable command for later MCP connections. They verify the original backup, unrelated configuration and unchanged registration after removal. The apt connection runs as the disposable non-root account. Native execution and public delivery remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Scoop, Homebrew, apt and Nix acceptance now checks real tool preferences and editable Shape PSD/PSB files at both native depths. The first installed build creates the fixtures. Each checked generation reopens them and compares source definitions, original-depth rendered samples and saved bytes. Removal checks file retention and records fixture hashes privately. The APT harness launches headlessly as its disposable non-root user. Native execution and public delivery remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Scoop, Homebrew and Nix now have first-install qualification modes alongside apt. A single authenticated definition runs the channel's native installation, update-ownership, launch, MCP, removal and user-file preservation checks. Homebrew still checks signing, Gatekeeper and the quarantined app, and Nix still verifies a native rebuild. First-install receipts leave upgrade and Nix rollback pending. Native execution and public delivery remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Photoshop raster masks stored alongside a supported no-op vector mask can open from the real user-mask channel. Import proves the rendered and real mask headers and native samples agree, including samples outside the layer. Resident and native PSD/PSB saves regenerate both standard channels from the editable mask and retain the vector record. Resident format 60 and native readback bind the paired layout. Regression source covers both depths, mixed compression, off-frame samples, mismatched plane protection and save readback. Receiving-editor acceptance remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Editable Photoshop import can retain a pathless vector mask whose empty initial fill is inverted to full coverage. An enabled global vector-mask flag is admitted only with a preserved no-op mask on the same layer. Both PSD writers retain the original records. Conventional import still compares the complete original-depth composite. Regression source covers both depths, PSD/PSB readback, tag order and refusal of masks with active coverage. Photoshop acceptance remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. A disk-backed document can paste an external static PNG from the clipboard directly into native tiles. The worker admits the encoded bytes and reuses the shared PNG validation, native precision and color/orientation processing. The 128 MiB encoded cap, native canvas geometry and source budgets apply. A matching retained copy still uses pixel verification before restoring its source position. Regression source covers a PNG above the bitmap decode budget, exact hidden RGB, 16-bit promotion, placement and refusal of malformed or animated clipboard PNGs. Installed clipboard acceptance remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Clipboard PNG encoding now writes rows at their original channel depth, using one conversion row for 16-bit samples. It enforces the 128 MiB limit as bytes are produced, including the Linux identity chunk. Disk-backed copy and cut check cancellation during PNG encoding and again before clipboard publication. Regression source covers exact 8-bit and 16-bit round trips, hidden RGB, cancellation and refusal at the encoded-byte boundary. The existing clipboard memory admission and installed platform acceptance still apply.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Clipboard bitmaps paste into disk-backed documents directly from decoded pixels. The shared image-paste transaction streams admitted rows into native tiles and preserves the observed destination, paste placement and original channel words. Bitmap paste no longer depends on the inline PNG command size limit. Regression source covers an 8-bit clipboard readback of an internal cut, restoring the trusted origin and exact native channels, including hidden RGB. Runtime and installed clipboard acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Full-resolution vertical pans with unchanged horizontal bounds can share complete display stripes from the last published committed frame. The renderer verifies the source generation, display mode, grid, color-profile binding and display status before reusing immutable rows. Partial and exposed bands render from the current source and the result carries the new viewport receipt. Active whole-image filters and staged effects follow the normal render path. Regression source compares the reused output with a fresh render at both depths and checks that horizontal and reduced-resolution crops do not reuse pins. Runtime pan latency remains unmeasured.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Ordinary full-resolution native pans can align a new crop to the displayed texture grid. Alignment keeps the visible region covered and adds at most 12.5% to the usual buffered pixel count within the existing preview limit. It falls back to the ordinary crop when the displayed grid changes, there is insufficient overlap, or the alignment would leave the canvas. Reduced quality and admission retries keep their existing crop rules. Regression source covers a vertical pan, repeat reuse, canvas edges, distant moves, the extra-pixel limit and a changed texture limit. Runtime pan latency remains unmeasured.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Native canvas panning skips rendering when the rotated canvas bounds are wholly outside the viewport. Pending preview work is cancelled and its late replies are isolated. Panning back in requests the current visible crop at the selected quality. Offscreen screenshots can capture the empty viewport after observing the current document generation. Regression source covers 16,000-square projects at both depths, late replies, rotation and return to full detail. Runtime timing remains pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. The shared tiled canvas painter skips meshes wholly outside the viewport and skips tile checks when the whole image fits. It preserves the transformed mesh and filtering coordinates of visible tiles. An optimized Windows CPU probe on an Intel Core i7-14650HX with egui 0.31.1 compared the same 2,286-tile geometry in a 1,200 × 900 viewport. Across 17 paired trials of 35 frames, median frame preparation and tessellation went from 932 to 106 µs for a zoomed view and 652 to 145 µs for a rotated, mirrored view. The fitted view went from 805 to 818 µs. All three produced identical tessellated meshes. The probe used small synthetic textures and measured CPU work. GPU and end-to-end latency acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Ordinary disk-backed canvas frames now use the same bounded texture uploader as streamed gesture frames, so default views can reuse matching textures during a crop change. Cached and newly materialized frames move their admitted stripes into the uploader. Completed texture sets still publish through the current preview receipt, and admitted fade baselines remain available. Regression source covers both depths, shifted tiles from materialized frames, and a display upload that retains its fade baseline. Runtime latency and native presentation acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Native streamed previews can reuse an uploaded texture at a different tile position or after a viewport resize when its outer dimensions and rendered RGBA hash match. The new viewport supplies the placement and sampling coordinates. A changed filtering border requires a new texture unless the exact new pixels already exist in another tile. The lookup stays within the native preview admission and existing renderer context. Regression source covers a crop that moves three textures and uploads one changed edge at both depths. Runtime pan and upload latency remain unmeasured.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Resident regional previews now transfer only the changed rectangle from the worker when the canvas displays its exact base frame. The receiver checks the base, dimensions, bounds and byte count before updating the CPU canvas and texture. Skipped or replaced bases, animation, and refinement use full frames. Regression source checks exact 8-bit and 16-bit display output and refusal of a patch for an unrelated same-size frame. Runtime latency remains unmeasured.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Resident preview cancellation now reaches both depths of mask preparation, including spatial feather rows and columns, and checks between layer effects and while building or updating their input rows. Regional mask preparation uses the same cancellation guard and reuses the full result when no prior mask exists. Interrupted preparation discards partial images and local preview caches. Regression source covers cancellation in a feather pass and exact uncancelled mask readback after retry. Individual color-effect kernels and in-flight GPU calls remain cancellation boundaries. Runtime latency remains unmeasured.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. The resident canvas supersedes pending previews when the document revision, preview resolution or isolated mask display changes. Resident CPU rows check cancellation during full and dirty rendering at both depths, including the unfiltered composite beneath whole-image filters and cross-tab previews. Cancelled dirty work clears its derived cache so the next frame rebuilds from the document. Compatible frames can still show progress during a continuous stroke. Individual effect kernels and in-flight GPU calls remain cancellation boundaries. Regression source covers row cancellation and exact full refresh after a cancelled dirty update. Runtime latency remains unmeasured.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Copy Merged uses the Shift modifier from the keypress, including when the shortcut is deferred until a selection commits. A regular Copy event keeps its ordinary meaning if Shift is pressed later in the frame. Native repeat suppression and text-field copying remain in place. Regression source distinguishes the two paths with an image filter and checks pasted pixels and position at both depths. Physical keyboard and installed clipboard acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Homebrew definition preparation can use an exact public signed DMG when the typed qualification binds it to the inspected notarized ZIP. The cask installs its root app, stable CLI and versioned source and notices. Homebrew ownership observation accepts both supported staging layouts and rejects duplicate matching links. The published 0.30.1 tag is an earlier commit. This source change awaits native acceptance and catalog delivery.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. The signed APT acceptance harness can now qualify the first repository generation independently, with a separate install receipt and upgrade explicitly pending. APT and Nix lifecycle checks require the observed installed build to identify the package manager as the update owner with in-app update eligibility disabled. Native acceptance and public channel delivery remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Selected Cut in Mask mode copies the raw paint step that Delete clears. The copied alpha follows the selection, holes contribute no pixels, and selected hidden RGB and original channel depth survive. The shared native target resolver now treats a null step as the default last paint step, matching the resident editor. Copy Merged uses the same filtered composite in Color and Mask mode, with the usual profile handling at each depth. Regression source covers explicit and default targets, offset geometry, feathering, disabled steps, sibling preservation, clipboard write refusal, PNG transport and matching merged copies. Runtime and installed clipboard acceptance remain pending.

Unreleased development update, 2026-11-10. Photoshop Color and Gradient Overlays can open as editable sources on non-Normal raster layers when Blend Interior Effects As Group is explicitly enabled. The loader validates `infx` after reading all layer tags, then requires an exact match to the saved composite.
