Unreleased source update, 2026-11-07: native existing-file saves now distinguish current-file access rules from backup rules. Windows current candidates preserve the original DACL inheritance state and exact supported descriptor, while backups keep their separate-directory inheritance protection. Supported Windows creation time and ordinary attributes are copied through held handles, with the current file marked for backup and its new write time retained. Metadata preflight runs before candidate encoding or external-publication journaling and is repeated against the held original/candidate before publication and settlement. Named streams, unsupported Windows attributes and visible Unix extended attributes/resource forks are explicitly refused until copying and accounting are connected; Linux access ACLs remain handled by the existing ACL path. Stream enumeration uses the actual volume capabilities and a bounded aligned native buffer, with checked offsets, names and lengths. Regression source covers inherited/protected DACLs, creation/property roundtrips and changes, malformed/duplicate/extra streams, real metadata refusal, unchanged original contents and early shared-save refusal. Windows compile-only checks passed across library, test and example targets, with warnings remaining; tests have not been executed, and the new Unix paths have not been compiled on this host. Audit/security-resource metadata, other platform properties and full metadata qualification, native recovery/remaining routes, ordinary native adoption, runtime/platform/release acceptance and the wider backlog remain unfinished.

Unreleased source update, 2026-11-07: shared native Save now routes both new destinations and existing-file replacements through the same Engine/source, cancellation, ownership and saved-name/history fence. Saving back to a loaded source requires its exact observed file version; replacing another destination requires explicit overwrite consent. The existing-file writer retains the original physical file under its journal-bound name, keeps the registered complete backup copy, and publishes the candidate with a no-clobber rename. Both directory identities, full original/candidate/backup hashes and all retained debt are checked before successful settlement. Late human destinations or changed publication facts retain every version and the pending admission without an overwriting rollback. Admission includes both retained original and backup directory capacity before external staging begins. Regression source covers native 8/16-bit PSD/PSB repeated saves, standard pixels, original physical identity and named-stream retention, exact closed debt, explicit replacement consent, external source changes, late foreign files and cancellation/newer human edits for new and existing destinations. Windows compile-only checks passed for library, test and example targets, with warnings remaining; tests have not been executed. Current candidate filesystem metadata copying remains limited to supported access rules; the original filesystem metadata stays on the retained physical file. Full metadata qualification, native recovery/registration/export and remaining routes, ordinary native adoption, runtime/large-canvas/platform/release acceptance and the wider backlog remain unfinished.

Unreleased source update, 2026-11-07: native existing-file save preparation now has a held backup-data component. It registers the exclusive zero-byte backup identity in the durable publication journal before access-rule changes or payload writes, copies the original main data stream under the existing frozen quota lock, verifies complete backup bytes and the original source hash, and transfers the completed backup into the bounded inventory exactly once. Cancellation, changed names and uncertain prefixes retain files and admitted debt. Regression source covers both document depths, exact bytes, duplicate inventory transfer, empty/partial cancellation, foreign name replacement and same-size/same-timestamp source edits. The shared no-clobber rename now supports two held directories on one volume, checks both names and syncs both sides; Windows pins both directory trees. The original physical file can be relocated without a copy/unlink fallback. External-publication intent version 3 binds a separate retained-original name; closed records, restart census, chain validation and destination protections preserve that exact original identity, including an absent target after a refused publication. Earlier intent formats are retained and refused rather than reinterpreted. Additional regression source covers physical identity/timestamps and Windows named streams, occupied destinations, cancellation/directory mismatch, fixed-record roundtrips, required successor retention and pending/closed restart debt. Existing-file staging now reuses the registered candidate replay and supported access-rule copy, holds the original file alongside its complete backup, and emits abort receipts covering original, backup, candidate and frozen historical debt. Incomplete candidates cannot settle their admitted growth; foreign source replacement and corrupt backups cannot produce a successful close. New native outputs suppress deferred Windows write-stamp updates and set an explicit completion time before their sealed facts are captured; their stamps remain checked after writing owners are released. Regression source includes actual 8/16-bit session candidates, both creation registrations, complete and cancelled aborts, foreign originals, same-stamp backup corruption and post-close file-version/fact consistency. Windows compile-only checking passed across library, test and example targets; tests have not been executed. This component does not authorize existing-file replacement or claim preservation of alternate streams, extended attributes, resource forks or audit rules. Existing-file native save remains refused; filesystem metadata handling, replacement/recovery integration, native authority activation, platform/runtime/release acceptance and the wider backlog remain unfinished.

Unreleased source update, 2026-11-07: native Save As now prepares an admitted filename-only document generation outside Engine, binds it to the exact native history identity/epoch, validates it before publication and installs it with the saved path/version only after the verified destination and journal settle. Artwork revision, source rasters and both history stacks remain intact; frozen captures retain their original metadata, and stale prepared work cannot publish against the new generation. A save also separates later gesture history. Native chronological undo/redo retain the current filename. Selective frame undo recognizes only captured save-name context; unrelated name changes and later human pixels/metadata retain their strict conflict checks. Refused name installation returns its original owner for off-lock retirement. Regression source covers both depths, real shared Save As with Unicode names and retained redo, frozen sources/records, same-name saves, namespace/cursor refusal, cancellation/admission cleanup and selective frame compensation with later-pixel protection. Existing-file replacement/backup outcomes, recovery, registration/export and remaining native routes still keep ordinary native adoption closed. Unreleased source update, 2026-11-07: the shared working-save lifecycle now routes private native authorities through the existing verified Source36 producer and closed destination/publication journal. Native Save As prepares standard native-depth layer/mask data, supplementary sources and the current merged composite, replays the fully verified candidate into an admitted temporary, and arms the exact save request under a memory-only Engine fence. Encoding, destination I/O, journal settlement and staging retirement run outside Engine. The route retains runtime file-ownership/path admission and does not change document pixels or history. Resident and native saves share exact document/revision-bound editability-loss consent, including early boolean validation and protected-document refusal. Finalization refusal now returns the real intent/closing owner for verified abort settlement instead of losing it at the cancellation fence; unresolved files remain charged. Unreleased source update, 2026-11-07: Engine now owns one document/history authority. Resident-only access is explicit and fallible; native views, scene capture, shared edit publication and chronological undo/redo use the same authoritative owner. Live native scene identity/reservation copies and state reports reserve bounded memory before copying, and display-profile/report preparation runs after releasing Engine. Project close retires the whole authority after releasing workspace/engine locks. Recovery defers on contention or source replacement; dirty native checkpoints explicitly report the unconnected publication route and retain earlier recoveries. User creation/open routes remain resident: native adoption stays private until save, recovery, registration and export are connected. Unreleased source update, 2026-11-07: Engine and proposal records no longer implement wholesale Clone. Frozen resident proposal documents have one shared immutable snapshot; canvas and MCP review use it directly, command arrays move into their record, and acceptance temporarily transfers the record without copying its commands or metadata. Refused acceptance restores its position and source owner, then rechecks expiry. Resident preview copies and revision restamping now run on the bounded worker; original review uses the current frame owner. Cross-project move rollback checkpoints destination document/history/edit presentation only, preserves live runtime/file/proposal ownership and cannot reacquire ended tasks. The source edit retains its own atomic failure handling. Rust embedding callers that previously cloned Engine must retain a captured scene or construct an explicit isolated document engine. Unreleased source update, 2026-11-07: held native proposals now expose source-only retirement batches for off-lock cleanup. Source retirement also binds the registered file/directory identities before deletion and before inventory release. Failures during or after unlink cannot acquire another retry token; the batch preserves an unresolved-inventory result instead of later reporting successful cleanup. Existing one-shot proposal helpers use the same batch path. Unreleased source update, 2026-11-07: native canvas endpoint and published-baseline caches now yield to resident-budget pressure through a bounded weak registration. A failed reservation tries nonblocking, memory-only eviction, then retries with the original strict capacity limit. Retirement drops both cache owners, invalidates captured fade generations and cancels presentation jobs; caching stays disabled for the affected source so a retry cannot rebuild the same pressure loop. Escaped frames and pending replies retain their real charges until released, and stale publication cannot restore a retired baseline. Full-quality sampling, source data and history remain unchanged. Unreleased source update, 2026-11-07: native raster AI fades are now connected to canvas capture, the bounded preview worker and successful texture publication. One actually published committed baseline and one worker endpoint retain admitted display pixels/selection metadata only; intermediate frames never become committed baselines or satisfy settled capture. Later fade steps reuse the current endpoint, with document/project/view/epoch and cache-generation guards. Human editing previews, preview-quality changes and project teardown retire derived caches. Cancellation is never swallowed as optional-cache refusal. Render/publication failures stop only native canvas animation, release caches and allow at most one cache-free retry per source; shared artwork/history and numeric/blue feedback remain intact. Unreleased source update, 2026-11-07: native scalar AI canvas steps now wait for actual publication of the matching request before advancing, preventing wall-clock UI sampling from repeatedly superseding a slow preview. Human interruption and the existing bounded highlight/transition lifecycle remain. A shared admitted native display-blend renderer now retains only preview pixel pins/tags, checks original/current generations, document/project/view/grid/profile bindings, blends straight-alpha display samples correctly, and gives intermediate results separate presentation provenance. The macOS updater omits package-managed configuration and rechecks ownership before native app checks, installed verification and retained-proof use. # PeerBrush v0.3 — development

Unreleased source update, 2026-11-07: PB-158 now includes a Nix source-package definition producer and reviewed derivation template for native x86_64-linux. It reuses exact public release/source verification, exports the actual Cargo.lock and portable support files, pins the source ZIP by its verified SHA-256 and Nixpkgs by an explicitly selected full Git revision, and rejects unpinned/Git/alternate-registry dependency records without an adapter. The derivation keeps the declared Rust pin and existing native JPEG XL qualifier, retains full corresponding source/dependency inputs in its closure, installs desktop/MIME/support data, requires compiler runtime notices and uses the wrapper layout handled by Nix MCP registration. Package-input metadata stays separate from the source build's unknown embedded Git provenance. The shared native tool selector now preserves the selected absolute compiler launcher rather than resolving away C++ driver aliases; file/version/timeout/privacy checks remain. Regression source covers real synthetic public source/lock/support bindings, immutable outputs, unsafe/mutable pins and POSIX driver alias behavior. Python/embedded-Python syntax, source-manifest and diff checks passed. No tests, Nix evaluation/build, toolchain qualification, reproducibility or installed/client/upgrade/rollback acceptance have run. No Nixpkgs revision is qualified and no package/catalog is published; distribution and application backlog work remain unfinished.

Unreleased source update, 2026-11-07: PB-158 Linux Nix MCP registration now keeps a conventional profile launcher instead of persisting a versioned store image. Candidate paths preserve native spaces/Unicode and are checked against the observed running executable or the known makeWrapper sibling in the same immutable object; missing, different, non-executable or changing targets refuse registration before client writes. The existing default-store classifier is shared with discovery, so previous Nix .peerbrush-wrapped entries can migrate through the normal backed-up TOML/JSON update path, while unrelated lookalikes and non-MCP servers remain preserved. Regression source covers profile layout/upgrade target changes, wrapper refusal, missing paths, exact client backups, existing settings and idempotent migration. Compile-only checking passed, including the shared classifier/client migration source; native Unix filesystem execution and tests have not run. Nix derivation/toolchain/source/dependency pins, actual launch/client/upgrade/rollback acceptance, custom-profile support, publication and the wider application backlog remain unfinished. No Nix package is claimed as delivered.

Unreleased source update, 2026-11-07: PB-158 Linux update ownership now recognizes process images inside valid default Nix store objects, including wrapped executables, as Nix-managed. The updates panel identifies Nix as the owner of upgrades and rollbacks; public release discovery still does not establish package-provider availability. Existing system/Flatpak routing is preserved, while an unavailable Linux executable observation stays Unknown instead of being guessed as Direct. The classifier invokes no package manager and reads no environment-supplied ownership declaration. Regression source covers wrapped/versioned store objects, malformed/lookalike/escaping paths, existing Linux owners and the actual update controls with unchanged 8/16-bit artwork/history. Existing source-archive provenance remains unknown without real Git metadata; no build-commit override was added. The remaining application and distribution backlog is still open.

Unreleased source update, 2026-11-07: PB-158 now includes source for attaching externally produced APT signatures to a fresh candidate. The attachment path re-inspects the DEB/portable/source plan, requires the exact canonical unsigned manifest and tree, and rechecks repository bytes after native verification. An explicitly selected/hash-pinned GnuPG executable runs in an isolated temporary home with public-certificate preflight, no default configuration, service autostart or key retrieval. InRelease and detached signatures must bind the same Release bytes and approved primary fingerprint; selected primary/signing keys and SHA-2 signatures must remain valid through Release expiry. Private material, weak/unsupported algorithms, ambiguous results and expiry during copying cannot produce a success receipt. Output preserves source/package bytes and all unverified native/publication gates.
