The supplied PB-122 regression has four records, including two zero-size `nvrt` adjustments with zero-size white-default masks, a Multiply raster at opacity 89/255 and a base raster. The global `Patt` payload is empty. Import work must connect adjustment composition, document-space masks and editable standard save/reopen, as well as classify/preserve the remaining global records. Its inactive/advisory global forms now have a standard preservation path. Actual composite matching, editable reopening and receiving-editor acceptance for this file remain unverified; tag recognition alone cannot close PB-122. See [Adobe's format specification](https://www.adobe.com/devnet-apps/photoshop/fileformatashtml/) for adjustment and global-record definitions. Reference artwork remains outside source control.

The 0.30.0 release retains the existing canvas limits. The live 16000 × 16000
editor (PB-068) moves to the next version. Its preserved work and measurements
below remain available for that milestone.

Native preview rows (unreleased) now retain admitted display stripes independently
of their captured source session. Prepared providers preserve the existing 8/16-bit
preview sampling grid, target semantics, mask values and display projection. ICC
display handling, pointwise colour effects and finite-support Blur/Bloom are connected;
global styles have an operation-owned stage path; Liquify and its retained soft clips use typed mapping/source preparation; editable sources still fail explicitly. Live native routing
and complete provider qualification remain integration gates.

Native presentation uses bounded stock-egui texture tiles with neighboring-pixel
halos and cropped coordinates. Conversion and replacement owners share the native
admission pool. A retirement owner keeps each upload image until all framework
and renderer image owners release it, then destroys its pixels before refunding
their charge. Complete candidate textures replace the preceding canvas together;
resource, cancellation and stale-request refusals preserve the preceding frame.
This accounts for owned CPU upload buffers, not opaque graphics-driver memory or
total process RAM. Native graphics acceptance remains required.

November 1 native-source foundations (unreleased): explicit captured scenes,
fallible prepared regions, native tile edit kernels and complete bounded Source36
container verification are being integrated. Source36 has its own strict metadata,
bulk-tile and standard-byte bindings; legacy source formats remain independent.
Unpublished staging fragments can retire only after their complete known prefix,
identity and ownership are verified. Published or uncertain fragments retain their
bytes and quota. Native composition keeps the existing precision of clipping,
pass-through folders, fractional masks and disabled-mask serialization. These
components do not raise the live 8192-edge/32MP limits. Complete large-document
editing, history, recovery, standard save/reopen and native workspace acceptance
remain required before unrestricted 16000-square readiness can be claimed.

October 31 distribution scope (unreleased): Windows uses Microsoft Store
installation and Store-managed updates. Standalone Windows signing, App Installer
and the dormant direct-update lifecycle are separate historical work, not release
prerequisites for this channel. Store-only final composition and bounded upstream
acquisition retain exact source/package/report bindings alongside Mac/Linux
qualification. The publisher's same-invocation Store handoff remains disabled;
genuine Store producer, installed/upgrade and authenticated public-availability
qualification are still required. No production workflow, credential or service
configuration has been activated. The existing 8192-edge/32MP limits remain;
unrestricted 16000-square support and PB-108 native acceptance remain open.

Selection flood visitation uses byte generations with a full clear before a
generation is reused. Its payload is one byte per pixel, versus four bytes for
the previous full-width stamps; this is not a total process RAM bound. Existing
selection admission and work limits remain unchanged. Repeated short seeds and
generation rollover must preserve the same native selection, source and history
semantics; full performance and large-document qualification remain open.

October 29 setup follow-through (unreleased): a separate disabled fixed-offer
setup target now connects exact signed local media to fresh registration and
source-bound installed-identity observation. Its producer requires actual
source/media/native-origin binding, builds static CRT, verifies native metadata
and refuses runtime offer injection. A setup-only Windows x64 origin adapter
preserves the aggregate verifier's Linux-only policy and its exact pinned
OIDC/workflow/job checks. Identity proof is deliberately not installed acceptance
or update/website eligibility. No signing, installation, production activation or
publication follows from source/component checks. Genuine signed qualification,
the documented prior-attempt/reinstall hold and provider-dependent signing and
installed/public adapters remain open. Current test evidence belongs on draft PR
#49; earlier checkpoint totals below do not qualify this batch.

October 28 reconstruction (unreleased): capture dequeue now holds nonblocking root/selected-engine and registry guards through the painted document/revision/target and pending-panel checks. The installed Ubuntu GUI fixture acknowledges its deliberate duplicate-open warning through a bounded visible primary-button detector in the authenticated focused window, retaining its original canvas density, source and history oracles. Numeric legacy tile input rejects the first excess byte before collecting the remainder; compact base64 compatibility remains.

Ordinary native8/16 working Save now renders approximately 128 KiPixel composite strips into a third exclusive owned append-only scratch file, then verifies SHA-256 strip bindings while replaying standard planar channels into the existing spool. ICC, bake and effect paths retain their materialized behavior. The 8192-edge/32MP caps and 256 MiB per-staging-file limit remain: three files can require up to 768 MiB temporary disk, plus destination and retained previous versions. Bounded strip buffers are not a total application RAM guarantee or a speed claim. PB-108 and unrestricted 16000-square PB-068 acceptance remain open.

Retained Windows recovery observation now independently authenticates the previous signed CURRENT archive against the initial-profile/transition binding while NEW executes, retaining leases, bytes and inventory rechecks. macOS contradictory/orphan native markers refuse profile migration and repeat app/profile/admission/absence checks. These guards grant no apply, launch-health, restore or cache-retirement authority. Windows superscript COM/LPT aliases are rejected before opening an archive destination, and nested packaging fixtures register cleanup before setup can fail.

The lost October 27 local-only commits were not recovered; reconstructed source has new commits and requires new native evidence. Local Python checks and exact-head CI status are recorded on draft PR #49. Historical counts and benchmarks below do not validate this reconstruction. Production activation and publication remain disabled; the Windows pre-install digest/trust decision is resolved in favor of exact local bytes; implementation qualification and signed-package/hardware acceptance remain open.

Current implementation batch (unreleased, 2026-10-24): Save borrows its immutable document and buffers JSON fragments before guarded compression, avoiding repeated shared-engine lock acquisition for tiny values. The completed comparison below measures the combined Save implementation.

Windows stable discovery verifies exact immutable descriptor bytes separately from CURRENT recovery media, validates each installed version's actual association and includes the matching producer/publication alias. Native staging now preserves an initial allowlisted default-settings copy without holding later human preference writes. Guarded macOS planned Quit connects authenticated source/profile checks to the atomic writer-sealing close transaction; parent/owner-death observations preserve the recovery hold. The macOS app now encloses its corresponding source and a pre-signing identity inside signed resources, so drag-to-Applications retains them. The retained native Installed callback connects to actual signed target/source inspection rather than accepting version or callback claims alone.

Activation remains disabled; final Windows exit/apply, launch health, compatible recovery and repeated upgrade/cache transitions remain unfinished. The proposed Windows stable filename is `PeerBrush-Windows-x64.appinstaller`; no feed is published or configured. PB-108 remains first and open for native input/presentation acceptance. Remote control and add-ons remain excluded.

This checkpoint began after the 0.26.0 Mac/Linux release. PeerBrush 0.4 carries the original acceptance criteria, earlier verified work and unresolved reports forward. [PeerBrush 0.4 backlog](../FOLLOWUPS.MD#peerbrush-04--remaining-development-and-acceptance) · [0.26 record](development-0.26.md)

A Windows pass does not establish the Ubuntu cause or a production fix. The full suite and completed Save comparison were not repeated for this updater increment.

The next installation slice is `c242d03` (initial Windows preference preservation) and `6883710` (signed macOS installed identity/source and connected native inspection). Its three affected packaging modules ran 75 cases: 64 passed, eight failed the same new identity-byte check and three were skipped. ## Latest Save comparison (unreleased)

On Windows, the normal development executable at `10a4eeab4e76052085616be515ca264907712a51` was compared with the preserved original `2de8c0dcff5e31b8e1968e1a12a0ad3d9fb83c0a` executable. Sixteen trials used the same dense 1024-square native8/16 fixtures, retained transforms, Undo/Redo and synchronous shared-engine Save; each cell had two observations per build with reversed pair order. Fixture creation, editing and readback were outside the Save timer.

| Document | Original median | Current median | Reduction |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 8-bit PSD | 7.19 s | 0.83 s | 88.4% |
| 8-bit PSB | 7.10 s | 0.90 s | 87.3% |
| 16-bit PSD | 14.26 s | 1.77 s | 87.6% |
| 16-bit PSB | 14.27 s | 1.58 s | 88.9% |

All 16 trials preserved native pixels, complete editable/retained sources, current standard composites and history/reservations; independent reopening/resaving, stale-request refusal, file ownership, original bytes and owned-process closure passed. These results cover the combined implementation, not the isolated contribution of each optimization. Both builds used the existing development profile; two observations, uncontrolled caches/scheduling and headless execution limit interpretation. Sampled private memory was mixed, including a higher 8-bit PSB sample; observed sampling gaps reached about 18–22 ms despite the requested 5 ms interval. No universal memory reduction, native input latency, signed installed behavior or 16000-square acceptance follows.

The completed earlier `877f89b` batch recorded 1,335 Rust passes, one failure and 22 ignored across 96 targets, with three explicit local provider exclusions; 557 Python passes, one failure and 15 skips across 573 unique methods; and all 18 headless application cases passed. Both failures were obsolete Windows discovery fixtures: the canceled first transfer now retains `discovery.appinstaller`, and the prepared inventory includes the stable descriptor. Commit `ba18069` corrects them. At `10a4eea`, the affected 34 PSD/PSB, 16 shared-Save and one cancellation checks passed, as did the corrected Python packaging case, formatting, normal build and repository-configured Clippy. Four ignored diagnostics in those focused Rust groups were not run. The full suite was not repeated; earlier failures and skips are not reclassified as current full-suite passes.

CI562 failed the shared cancellation fixture on all three platforms. Earlier CI560 completed Windows and macOS jobs, while Ubuntu failed its disposable installed-GUI operation after real historical-portable migration passed. The Ubuntu cause remains unknown; four fixed parent-operation messages identify the failing step on the next native run without exposing private stderr. The affected installed-GUI script contracts passed nine checks with one POSIX-only skip; this is not actual installed desktop acceptance. Current native CI and physical desktop/Photoshop acceptance remain required. Physical desktop verification has not run for this source. No update installation, publication, deployment or Store submission occurred.

## Scope and order

The implementation scope now proposes **v0.3 inclusion of PB-145/PB-146/PB-147/PB-155/PB-154**, explicitly extending the earlier release-unassigned plan. Sequence these foundations after the first measurable PB-108 fix: persistent settings/migration; platform installation and consent-based file associations; channel-specific authenticated updates and safe restart; then protected production publishing and Store reconciliation. Independent packaging work may proceed alongside the performance investigation. Inclusion is a development target, not a release-readiness claim. See [the release plan](release-signing-plan.md#high-priority-installation-and-production-foundations).

1. **PB-108 remains priority #1:** reproduce and measure startup and input-to-display delay, including the blank canvas, controls, navigation and real hardware. Preserve the earlier verified fixes and comparable baselines; prior CPU benchmarks do not close end-to-end latency acceptance.
2. **PB-122 remains #2:** conservative editable Photoshop imports and styled-source acceptance, with exact current standard-composite matching and protected unsupported originals.
3. **PB-110 remains #3:** the reported selection recurrence across copy/paste, selected transforms and cancellation. Earlier verified work does not close the recurrence.
4. Preserve the next relative order: recovery/lifecycle PB-080/PB-138, canvas/tablet ergonomics PB-072, then production export and receiving-application round trips PB-118/PB-070.
5. Other remaining work retains its detailed backlog criteria: keyboard layouts PB-023; smaller-model MCP discovery PB-043; text/OS fonts PB-066/PB-141; 16000 × 16000 documents PB-068; effects/clipboard/style acceptance PB-106/PB-133/PB-136; mask-link interchange PB-152; sliders and JXL/WebP PB-142/PB-143/PB-144; Info/menu/popups/clipboard canvas defaults PB-148–151; Free Transform/Warp PB-153; visual refinement PB-007/PB-042; historical-reference audit PB-139. PB-139's destructive-history actions still require separate explicit owner approval.

Standing precision, UI/MCP/CLI consistency, save safety and documented GPU/Photoshop fallbacks remain in force. Earlier completed rows and measurements remain historical records; only unresolved portions are scheduled here. Do not repeat implemented work or mark remaining native/hardware/receiving-application acceptance complete without evidence.

Remote control from another computer and third-party plugin/add-on tools are outside the requested scope and will not be developed. Historical placeholders are not a commitment to implement them in a later release. Existing local MCP/proposal work remains in scope. Finish the editor, installation and updates in this milestone.

## Implementation and review

The milestone and branch namespace remain v0.3; the approved application release version is **0.30.0** because SemVer orders 0.3.0 below published 0.26.0. Development packages use 0.30.0-dev.1. Keep Windows package versions and updater comparisons monotonic; never present development candidates as published upgrades.

`version/0.3` starts from synchronized `dev` and `main` history. Focused `feature/0.3` and `fix/0.3` branches target it under the [branch policy](development-guide.md#branches-and-pull-requests). The original planning commit from PR #32 is retained through a merge, without rewriting or duplicating its content. Audit earlier implementations against current shared code before porting anything.

Current implementation is consolidated directly on `version/0.3`, preserving the focused development history. Complete and integrate the current source batch before its combined regression and native QA pass. Earlier focused results remain checkpoint evidence; they do not establish acceptance of later combined changes. Production promotion and publication gates remain in force.

Use exact tested commits and applicable engine/codec/protocol checks plus native visual/hardware evidence for behavior changes, preserving native 16-bit words, current PSD/PSB composites, history and human work. Keep release publication, deployment, Store submission, protected signing approvals and destructive history operations subject to their own explicit authorization.

## Initial audit

The shared 0.26 code already contains retained first-input rendering, same-frame navigation, immediate human controls, Windows low-latency presentation, guarded native file activation, MSIX packaging/associations and stable installed MCP aliases. Existing brush, shortcut, filter and export preferences persist. Apple signing/notarization/stapling exists for ZIPs. Reuse these implementations; their remaining installed/native acceptance stays open.

PB-108 investigation begins with smoothed stroke tails: appending input changes the preceding final dab, causing the current replay session to discard all accumulated coverage. Measure the same release-mode workloads before and after a focused fix, assert exact native8/16 output and unchanged-prefix retention, then verify the native workspace. Full dab generation, OS delivery, worker scheduling, GPU presentation and physical tablet latency remain distinct measurements.

## PB-108 changed-tail replay

The shared brush session now finds the exact unchanged dab prefix. Reshaped/removed tails restore only their affected tile region from the original source and replay all current dabs contributing to that region in their original order. Other tiles and coverage remain retained. Full validation and stroke work limits still run before mutation. Tails that change or overlap the whole stroke still require that work; dab generation and validation remain linear in total stroke length.

Release-mode CPU measurements on Windows, Intel Core i7-14650HX, using `cargo run --locked --offline --release --example input_performance` and a 1536-edge preview:

| 2048² canvas, smoothing 0.35 | Before median / p95 | After median / p95 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Native8, 120 continuing inputs | 3.96 / 8.29 ms | 2.26 / 3.60 ms |
| Native16, 120 continuing inputs | 8.41 / 17.66 ms | 5.92 / 11.36 ms |

The before run used the unchanged shared brush implementation at the initial planning merge. A paired, same-process 2048×96 smoothed/tapered 120-input brush-only workload compares complete replay with the retained session and asserts exact output after every input: native8 median/p95 6.56/12.22 ms versus 1.21/2.48 ms; native16 14.80/49.99 ms versus 3.45/8.88 ms. Retained replay processes 4,244 contributing dabs versus 18,606. These samples include normal scheduling variation; they measure CPU work, not physical input-to-display latency or a GPU improvement.

All 29 focused brush-session/equivalence/texture/native16-paint/preview tests passed. New structural coverage verifies exact native8/16 source parity, pressure, textured tips, quantized opacity/flow, erase, polygon/rectangle clipping, taper, tail shrink/re-expansion and retained prefix tile identity. The complete local run passed 923 Rust tests with eight opt-in checks ignored, plus formatting, all-target Clippy with existing warnings and 21 packaging/privacy checks. Native Windows rendered workspaces showed the actual smoothed stroke at both depths; independent PNG decoding verified every original sample and exact undo/redo, and independent PSD/PSB merged-plane decoding plus editable reopening matched the current artwork exactly. Generated QA files stay outside committed source.

Exact-head Windows/macOS/Linux CI remains separate. Local MSIX tests passed 23 checks; their native SDK validation failed on the installed 10.0.18362 SDK, below the documented 10.0.22000 requirement. No manifest requirement was weakened. Startup, controls/navigation stalls, physical tablet latency and user acceptance remain open; this slice does not close PB-108.

## Consolidated source acceptance

The source batch following `b55fada` adds lossless WebP export (PB-144), connected main menus (PB-149), compact popup presentation (PB-150) and asynchronous clipboard canvas-size suggestions (PB-151). The UI and clipboard pieces selectively reuse the preserved workspace/acceptance branches, retaining their history and omitting obsolete version/completion claims. File → Export WebP explicitly labels the derived 8-bit output; PNG presets retain their previous default. WebP does not reduce native16 project samples or replace layered saves.

New Canvas owns document shortcuts from the opening frame through dismissal, drops delayed suggestions after manual focus/edit or cancellation, and creates a blank project only after confirmation. Windows clipboard transport acceptance is explicit and runs inside fresh private window stations; inaccessible isolation fails closed. CI runs those ignored native checks only after ordinary regression tests pass. Source review has not executed these checks.

This batch also includes the earlier About panel, bounded public website/download readback and empty bidi-run filename-bounds correction. Complete source integration and independent review precede one combined formatting, engine/codec/protocol, package and native QA phase. Record that phase's exact source commit and results separately; earlier passing checkpoints do not establish acceptance of the current batch. Signing, publishing, Store submission and automatic update installation remain disabled or approval-gated as documented.

The seven native GPU checks subsequently passed explicitly. Native codec calls run only in a source-matched child before application initialization. Strict bounded framing, nonce/build matching, checked native8/16 lengths, stop-aware pipe pumps, one-child ownership, deadline and platform limits protect failures. Unreapable children disable further codec work. macOS footprint monitoring is a watchdog, not a hard memory ceiling. This is fault isolation, not a security sandbox or total process/application OOM guarantee. Working Save now shares row serialization for native8/16 standard layer/mask channels and the current merged composite, using a caller-owned empty seekable spool. Existing public Vec APIs wrap the same core. Exact PSD/PSB lengths, padding, channel order, hidden RGB, native masks, white-matte composite and separate layer/composite FNV hashes are retained; supplementary source formats are unchanged. Pixel-transfer scratch uses a fixed 16 KiB row plus a 64 KiB buffered transfer/copy, with the write buffer dropped before copying. Metadata, full render/effect/mask preparation and compressed private sources still allocate separately.

Original working-save publication retains source identity/revision/path/file-version/saved-revision guards, destination lease, previous exact bytes, ownership preparation, actual-write fingerprints, held/named regular-file identity and verified published bytes before saved-state promotion. Spool/final files are separately exclusive and capped at 256 MiB each. Partial writes, source changes, cancellation and foreign temporary replacements reject publication; post-rename failure keeps current work open and previous bytes retained. An unidentifiable new temporary is left with an explicit orphan warning rather than unsafe deletion.

Three codec methods prepare independent literal standard bytes at one/two pixels, native8/16 PSD/PSB/editable/standard-only reopening, bounded short writes, corrupted/truncated spool, row/copy cancellation and disk-write failure. Two portable Save methods cover native channels/history/ownership/previous versions and pre/post-publication fault matrices; a Unix method additionally protects same-held-inode symlink replacements. The existing source-race method now exercises all five source fields. Parent review corrected an initially unchanged saved-marker injection before execution; parent and independent final source review passed. Combined/runtime/native filesystem acceptance follows this complete batch.

No dependency, file/admission cap or source-format change is made. This is a standard pixel-transfer foundation, not a total-RAM bound or PB-068 completion: 256 MiB files, 8192-pixel edges, 32-million-pixel admission, full preparation, decoding and recovery limits remain. The two temporary files may require up to 512 MiB extra disk space in addition to destination/previous versions. Native desktop and real receiving-application acceptance remain separate.

## Measured Save memory/time tradeoff (unreleased)

A separate reviewed Windows diagnostic compared the retained exact-hash 23d main with 28b after local qualification finished. Both use Rust 1.92, the same optimized dev profile (application 1/dependencies 2, debug info disabled), Clang 21.1.8 and unchanged build inputs. Twenty-four fresh owned processes measured three paired repetitions for each native depth and PSD/PSB output, reversing binary order per case across repetitions. Each opened the same clean dense 2048 by 2048 one-raster PSD and used original Save As to an absent path, avoiding dirty recovery serialization. All trials passed observed source/history/header/hash checks and cleanup.

| Native depth / output | Median sampled Save private commit, 23d to 28b | Median Save wall time, 23d to 28b |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8 / PSD | 208.0 to 66.0 MiB | 845 to 1151 ms |
| 8 / PSB | 209.9 to 65.9 MiB | 842 to 1137 ms |
| 16 / PSD | 514.6 to 196.4 MiB | 1308 to 1928 ms |
| 16 / PSB | 513.8 to 164.8 MiB | 1299 to 1949 ms |

Private commit is not resident RAM. These sampled current Save-phase peaks are lower bounds: requested 5 ms sampling had a 17 ms maximum gap and at least 56 Save samples per trial. Lifetime peaks were recorded separately; import already dominates or nearly dominates the new process high water. Wall time includes ordinary CLI startup/request overhead. The controller/CLI processes, OS cache and system commit are excluded, scheduling/caches remain uncontrolled and three repetitions leave a 2:1 per-case order imbalance. Exact large-fixture pixel equality is not proved by this diagnostic; separate codec and small native app acceptance supply their own fidelity evidence.

The reduction is material, but Save was 34-50 percent slower in this workload. Investigation continues before any Save-speed or PB-108 latency improvement claim. The spool transfer retains full preparation/private-source allocations, extra disk use and all document caps; no total-memory bound, 16k support or PB-068 completion is claimed.

No image, Save or export warmed that edited document before its first timed request.

| Shared CPU preview route | Baseline median (ms) | Candidate median (ms) |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Blur, first request after edit | 1,547.33 | 998.32 |
| Bloom, first request after edit | 1,679.19 | 1,096.81 |
| Blur, median of session warm medians | 233.94 | 232.40 |
| Bloom, median of session warm medians | 271.81 | 263.47 |

First-request time was **35.5% lower for blur and 34.7% lower for bloom**; warm requests changed little. Timing includes CLI/IPC, shared `Document::preview`, PNG encoding/delivery and owned preview-file publication. Independent decoding after timing matched every delivered preview to the native PNG projection and required the effect to differ from the plain reference. All before/after trials matched decoded preview, native effect, original native tiles/hidden RGB/low bits and current standard merged-composite hashes, with exact source/history, ownership refusal and native undo protection. Actual candidate blur and bloom previews were visually inspected. Two earlier private-driver checks failed before timed trials, were independently corrected and remain excluded from the completed measurement.

The separate balanced 2048-square Open comparisons each completed 16 trials with every native-pixel, PNG, source/history, ownership and standard-composite oracle passing. PSD medians were 406.38 to 398.01 ms at 8 bit and 655.43 to 651.37 ms at 16 bit. PNG medians were 186.06 to 181.63 ms and 279.06 to 276.51 ms. These small differences do not establish a further Open responsiveness improvement. Requested 5 ms process-memory samples had gaps up to 16.79 ms; no material sampled-memory reduction or total-peak claim follows. Four old-first source-JSON diagnostic cells also completed, with mixed small differences rather than a balanced application result. No Save-memory reduction is measured for bounded supplementary-resource transfer.

Every current-build observation was slower, in both order positions. An independent readback verified the 16 unique trials, saved-file hashes, native/source/composite signatures, paired order and summaries.

Native16 working-set medians increased. No universal memory reduction, physical input improvement or dense16000 qualification is claimed.

## Published 0.30.1 installation hotfix

The hotfix adds a native Finder drag-to-Applications layout with Ember artwork and a first-launch PSD/PSB default-editor choice. The choice is stored outside document history and can be reopened from File. macOS requests both types through NSWorkspace only from the installed app after consent; Windows opens Default apps, and Linux requests the registered desktop handler. Not now preserves the existing defaults. Normal user launches show the prompt; explicit development workspaces and update recovery do not introduce it. [Published release](https://github.com/Deftr0y/PeerBrush/releases/tag/v0.30.1)
